ſaw at the Sign of the Frock in Axc-Yard, and they
may reccive full ſatisfaiion. F

and puts upon their Backs Senatorian Robes, and

Jeſt. You hinder my Story with your Simile's.
He was dealt withal, and they told him ſeriouſly;
Sir T. ſay they, you grow old, and though we
doubt not but you wiſh well to our Deſigns, yet
to tell you the Truth, you ſignifie no more than a
Dog in a Coach-box, who looks the ſame way,
and as wiſely as the Coachman, but drives not at
al: Theretore we think it neceſſary that you lay
down, and though the Place you hold, oblige you
to do Nothing; yet, to ſerve the righteous Cauſe,
you may with a ſafe Conſcience ſay, you are un-
able to do That. |

Earn. He muſt be very feeble indeed, that can't
ſo much as do Nothing. | |

Jefl. Well! A Letter is dreſt up for him to in-
torm, that Age, and the concomitant Infirmities,
had incapacitated him for this weighty Charge, and
he prays Liberty to recede.

Earn. He got leave, no doubt. |

7ft, Li tell you. The Leaders of the Faction
